# Get transactions from the last 8 weeks excluding current week
# Current week starts at the most recent start of week, so we need to go back 1 week first
# then get 8 weeks worth of data
transactions_info = Transactions.WHERE(
    # Transaction date is between 9 weeks ago and 1 week ago
    (date_time >= DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-9 weeks')) &
    (date_time < DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-1 week'))
).CALCULATE(
    # Calculate week start date for grouping
    week_start = DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    # Check if transaction occurred on weekend (Saturday=7, Sunday=1 based on start_of_week config)
    is_weekend = (DAYNAME(date_time) == "Saturday") | (DAYNAME(date_time) == "Sunday")
)

# Group by week and calculate counts
result = transactions_info.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week_start = week_start,
    total_transactions = COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions = SUM(Transactions.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.ASC())

# Step 1: Define the time boundaries. We calculate the start of the current week
# and the start of the 8th week prior to establish our date range.
time_boundaries = Broker.CALCULATE(
    start_of_this_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    start_of_8_weeks_ago=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-8 weeks')
)

# Step 2: Filter transactions to include only stocks within the specified date range.
# For each relevant transaction, we determine its week's start date and whether it occurred on a weekend.
relevant_transactions = time_boundaries.Transactions.WHERE(
    (ticker.ticker_type == 'stock') &
    (date_time >= start_of_8_weeks_ago) &
    (date_time < start_of_this_week)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYNAME(date_time), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# Step 3: Partition the filtered transactions by their week's start date to group them weekly.
weekly_groups = relevant_transactions.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=(week_start_date))

# Step 4: For each weekly group, calculate the total transaction count and the count of weekend transactions.
# The results are ordered by the week's start date.
result = weekly_groups.CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=week_start_date,
    total_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions=SUM(IFF(Transactions.is_weekend, 1, 0))
).ORDER_BY(week_start_date.ASC())

# First, we filter the transactions to include only those made by customers from the USA
# and that occurred last week. The week is defined as starting on Sunday.
last_week_transactions_usa = Transactions.WHERE(
    (customer.country == "USA") &
    (date_time >= DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-7 days')) &
    (date_time < DATETIME('now', 'start of week'))
)

# Then, we calculate the total number of these transactions and the sum of their amounts.

    number_of_transactions=COUNT(last_week_transactions_usa),

# Filter transactions for buy transactions in the past 10 days
buy_transactions_past_10_days = Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "buy") & 
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME("now")) <= 10)
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol = ticker.symbol
)

# Group by ticker symbol and count transactions
ticker_buy_counts = buy_transactions_past_10_days.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_groups", 
    by=ticker_symbol
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol = ticker_symbol,
    num_buy_transactions = COUNT(Transactions)
)

# Get the top 2 most frequently bought tickers
result = ticker_buy_counts.TOP_K(2, by=num_buy_transactions.DESC())

# Get customers who joined in the last 6 months excluding current month
recent_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("months", join_date, DATETIME("now")) <= 6) & 
    (DATEDIFF("months", join_date, DATETIME("now")) >= 1)
).CALCULATE(
    join_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, "0"))
)

# Partition by join month to count signups and calculate average transaction amounts
result = recent_customers.PARTITION(name="months", by=join_month).CALCULATE(
    month=join_month,
    PMCS=COUNT(Customers),
    PMAT=AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Define the start and end dates for the 6-month period excluding the current month.
start_date = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')
end_date = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')

# Filter customers who joined within the specified date range.
recent_customers = Customers.WHERE((join_date >= start_date) & (join_date < end_date))

# Add a 'signup_month' column in 'YYYY-MM' format to be used for grouping.
customers_by_month = recent_customers.CALCULATE(
    signup_month=JOIN_STRINGS('-', YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, '0'))
)

# Partition the filtered customers by their signup month and calculate the required metrics.
result = customers_by_month.PARTITION(name="monthly_signups", by=(signup_month)).CALCULATE(
    month=signup_month,
    PMCS=COUNT(Customers),
    PMAT=AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)
)

)

        SUM(sell_transactions_last_month.amount) > 0,

        0.0
    )

# Step 1: Extract month and symbol from daily prices
price_info = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    month = JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date), LPAD(MONTH(date), 2, "0")),
    symbol = ticker.symbol,
    close_price = close,
    high_price = high,
    low_price = low
)

# Step 2: Group by symbol and month to calculate statistics
monthly_stats = price_info.PARTITION(name="months", by=(symbol, month)).CALCULATE(
    symbol = symbol,
    month = month,
    avg_close = AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    max_high = MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_low = MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 3: Calculate MoMC using PREV window function
result = monthly_stats.CALCULATE(
    symbol = symbol,
    month = month,
    avg_close = avg_close,
    max_high = max_high,
    min_low = min_low,
    momc = (avg_close - PREV(avg_close, by=(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC()), per="symbol")) / PREV(avg_close, by=(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC()), per="symbol")
).ORDER_BY(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC())

# Step 1: Create a view on DailyPrices to include a formatted month string (YYYY-MM) and the ticker symbol.
price_info = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date), LPAD(MONTH(date), 2, "0")),
    symbol=ticker.symbol
)

# Step 2: Partition the daily prices by symbol and month to calculate monthly aggregates.
# This calculates the average closing price, highest price, and lowest price for each ticker for each month.
monthly_stats = price_info.PARTITION(name="monthly_groups", by=(symbol, month)).CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month,
    avg_close=AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    max_high=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_low=MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 3: Partition the monthly statistics by symbol to facilitate the month-over-month calculation.
# Then, for each month within a ticker's group, calculate the final fields.
# The PREV window function is used to get the average closing price from the previous month for the same ticker,
# which is then used to compute the MoMC (Month-over-Month Change).
result = monthly_stats.PARTITION(name="ticker_groups", by=symbol).monthly_groups.CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month,
    avg_close=avg_close,
    max_high=max_high,
    min_low=min_low,
    momc=(avg_close - PREV(avg_close, by=month.ASC(), per="ticker_groups")) / PREV(avg_close, by=month.ASC(), per="ticker_groups")
)

prices_in_range = DailyPrices.WHERE(
    (date >= "2023-04-01") & (date <= "2023-04-04")
)

# Group by ticker and calculate max high and min low for each ticker
ticker_price_changes = prices_in_range.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_groups", 
    by=ticker_id
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=DailyPrices.ticker.symbol.SINGULAR(),
    max_high=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_low=MIN(DailyPrices.low),
    price_change=MAX(DailyPrices.high) - MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Get the top 3 tickers by price change
result = ticker_price_changes.TOP_K(
    3, 
    by=price_change.DESC()
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol,
    price_change
)

# First, create a view of transactions that includes the customer's state and the ticker's type.
transaction_details = Transactions.CALCULATE(
    customer_state=customer.state,
    ticker_type=ticker.ticker_type
)

# Next, partition these transactions by the combination of customer state and ticker type.
# For each combination, count the number of transactions.
combination_counts = transaction_details.PARTITION(
    name="combinations",
    by=(customer_state, ticker_type)
).CALCULATE(
    customer_state=customer_state,
    ticker_type=ticker_type,
    num_transactions=COUNT(Transactions)
)

# Finally, select the top 5 combinations with the highest number of transactions.
result = combination_counts.TOP_K(5, by=num_transactions.DESC())

# Filter for successful transactions in Q1 2023 and extract year/month
q1_2023_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (status == 'success') & 
    (YEAR(date_time) == 2023) & 
    (MONTH(date_time) >= 1) & 
    (MONTH(date_time) <= 3)
).CALCULATE(
    year = YEAR(date_time),
    month = MONTH(date_time)
)

# Partition by year and month, then calculate average price
result = q1_2023_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_groups", 
    by=(year, month)
).CALCULATE(
    year = year,
    month = month,
    avg_price = AVG(Transactions.price)
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC(), month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q32] Code generated by claude:
# Step 1: Get sales with sale price over $30,000 and their payments
high_value_sales = Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000)

# Step 2: Calculate week information for each payment
# Week starts on Sunday, so we need to find the start of each week
payment_info = high_value_sales.payment.CALCULATE(
    payment_date=payment_date,
    # Calculate the start of the week (Sunday) for each payment
    week_start=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    # Check if payment was made on weekend (Saturday=7, Sunday=1)
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date), (1, 7))
)

# Step 3: Calculate the date range for last 8 weeks excluding current week
# Current week start would be DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
# So we need weeks from 8 weeks ago to 1 week ago
date_range_info = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start=DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    eight_weeks_ago=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-8 weeks'),
    one_week_ago=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-1 week')
)

# Step 4: Filter payments to only include those in the last 8 weeks (excluding current)
filtered_payments = date_range_info.payment_info.WHERE(
    (week_start >= eight_weeks_ago) & (week_start <= one_week_ago)
)

# Step 5: Group by week and calculate totals
result = filtered_payments.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start,
    total_payments_received=COUNT(payment_info),
    weekend_payments_received=SUM(payment_info.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

    sale_month = DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month'),
    sale_price = sale_price,

    gpm = 100.0 * (
        SUM(Sales.WHERE(YEAR(sale_date) == 2023).sale_price) -

# Then, use these totals to compute the GPM according to the provided formula.

    total_revenue=SUM(sales_in_2023.sale_price),
    total_cost=SUM(sales_in_2023.car.cost)
).CALCULATE(
    gpm=((total_revenue - total_cost) / total_cost) * 100.0
)

# First, let's get all payments with year and month extracted
payments_with_month = PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    payment_year = YEAR(payment_date),
    payment_month = MONTH(payment_date),
    payment_amount = payment_amount
)

# Partition by year and month to get monthly totals
monthly_totals = payments_with_month.PARTITION(
    name="months", 
    by=(payment_year, payment_month)
).CALCULATE(
    year = payment_year,
    month = payment_month,
    total_payments = SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
)

# Calculate MoM change using PREV window function
result = monthly_totals.CALCULATE(
    year = year,
    month = month,
    total_payments_received = total_payments,
    mom_change = total_payments - PREV(
        total_payments, 
        by=(year.ASC(), month.ASC()),
        default=0
    )
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC(), month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q44] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Extract year and month from each payment record.
payment_info = PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    payment_year=YEAR(payment_date),
    payment_month=MONTH(payment_date)
)

# Step 2: Group by year and month, and sum the payment amounts for each group.
# This creates a new collection where each row represents a month that has payment data.
monthly_totals = payment_info.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_groups", by=(payment_year, payment_month)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_year=payment_year,
    payment_month=payment_month,
    total_payments=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
)

# Step 3: Calculate the Month-over-Month (MoM) change and format the output.
# The PREV function retrieves the total_payments from the previous month, ordered chronologically.
# For the first month in the dataset, PREV returns the specified default value. By setting
# default=total_payments, the MoM change for the first month correctly results in zero.
result = monthly_totals.CALCULATE(
    month=JOIN_STRINGS('-', payment_year, LPAD(payment_month, 2, '0')),
    total_payments=total_payments,
    mom_change=total_payments - PREV(total_payments, by=(payment_year.ASC(), payment_month.ASC()), default=total_payments)
).ORDER_BY(payment_year.ASC(), payment_month.ASC())

# First, let's get the payments from the previous ISO week
# We need to calculate the date range for the previous ISO week
# Start of current week (ISO weeks start on Monday)
current_week_start = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    current_date=DATETIME('now'),
    current_week_start=DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
)

# Filter payments for the previous week (7 days before current week start)
previous_week_payments = current_week_start.PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= DATETIME(current_week_start, '-7 days')) & 
    (payment_date < current_week_start)
)

# Group by payment date and payment method
result = previous_week_payments.PARTITION(
    name="daily_method_groups", 
    by=(payment_date, payment_method)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_date=payment_date,
    payment_method=payment_method,
    daily_total=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
).ORDER_BY(payment_date.ASC(), payment_method.ASC())

# Define the start and end dates for the previous ISO week
date_boundaries = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    start_of_last_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-7 days'),
    end_of_last_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-1 day')
)

# Filter payments to include only those from the previous week
previous_week_payments = date_boundaries.PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= start_of_last_week) & (payment_date <= end_of_last_week)
)

# Partition the filtered payments by date and method, then calculate the total amount for each group
result = previous_week_payments.PARTITION(
    name="daily_method_groups", by=(payment_date, payment_method)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_date=payment_date,
    payment_method=payment_method,

# Calculate DDD for each treatment where end_dt is not null
treatments_with_ddd = Treatments.WHERE(
    HASNOT(end_dt) == 0  # Ensure end_dt is not null
).CALCULATE(
    drug_name = drug.drug_name,
    ddd = tot_drug_amt / DATEDIFF("days", start_dt, end_dt)
)

    name="drug_groups",

[DEBUG] [Q71] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start of the current month to establish the upper bound of our date range.
start_of_current_month = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')

# Define the start of our 12-month period, which is 12 months prior to the start of the current month.
start_of_period = DATETIME(start_of_current_month, '-12 months')

# Filter the Treatments collection to include only records within the last 12 full months.
treatments_in_range = Treatments.WHERE(
    (start_dt >= start_of_period) & (start_dt < start_of_current_month)
)

# For each filtered treatment, calculate the start of the month to use for aggregation.
treatments_with_month = treatments_in_range.CALCULATE(
    treatment_month=DATETIME(start_dt, 'start of month')
)

# Partition the data by the calculated month, then for each month, calculate PMPD and PMTC.
# PMPD (per month patient diagnoses) and PMTC (per month treatment count) are both derived
# by counting the number of treatments within each monthly group.
result = treatments_with_month.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_groups", by=(treatment_month)
).CALCULATE(
    month=treatment_month,
    PMPD=COUNT(Treatments),
    PMTC=COUNT(Treatments)
).ORDER_BY(treatment_month.ASC())

# Step 1: For each patient, calculate the year of their first treatment
patients_with_first_year = Patients.WHERE(HAS(treatments_received) == 1).CALCULATE(
    patient_id=patient_id,
    first_treatment_year=YEAR(MIN(treatments_received.start_dt))
)

# Step 2: Group by year and count new patients
yearly_new_patients = patients_with_first_year.PARTITION(
    name="years", 
    by=first_treatment_year
).CALCULATE(
    year=first_treatment_year,
    number_of_new_patients=COUNT(Patients)
)

# Step 3: Calculate NPI (increase from previous year)
result = yearly_new_patients.CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    number_of_new_patients=number_of_new_patients,
    NPI=number_of_new_patients - PREV(
        number_of_new_patients, 
        by=year.ASC(), 
        default=0
    )
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC())

# For each drug, count adverse events that occurred in the same month as treatment start
drug_adverse_counts = Drugs.CALCULATE(
    drug_id=drug_id,
    drug_name=drug_name,
    num_adverse_events=SUM(
        treatments_used_in.adverse_events.WHERE(
            (YEAR(reported_dt) == YEAR(treatment.start_dt)) & 
            (MONTH(reported_dt) == MONTH(treatment.start_dt))
        ).CALCULATE(count=1).count
    )
)

# Find the drug with the highest number of such adverse events
result = drug_adverse_counts.TOP_K(1, by=num_adverse_events.DESC())

# Define the start of the 3-week period and the start of the current week to create a time window.
start_of_period = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-3 weeks')
start_of_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')

# 1. Start with notifications from users in the US or Canada.
# 2. Filter these notifications to include only those from the last three full weeks.
# 3. Calculate the specific week for each notification and determine if it occurred on a weekend.
notification_details = Users.WHERE(
    ISIN(country, ("US", "Canada"))
).notifications.WHERE(
    (created_at >= start_of_period) & (created_at < start_of_current_week)
).CALCULATE(
    notification_week=DATETIME(created_at, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYNAME(created_at), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# 4. Group the notifications by the calculated week.
# 5. For each week, count the total number of notifications and the number of weekend notifications.
result = notification_details.PARTITION(name="weekly_group", by=(notification_week)).CALCULATE(
    week=notification_week,
    total_notifications=COUNT(Notifications),
    weekend_notifications=SUM(is_weekend)
)

[DEBUG] [Q102] Code generated by gemini:
# This query calculates the Per Month Daily Active Users (PMDAU) for the last two months, excluding the current one.
# It first calculates the Daily Active Users (DAU) and then averages these values per month.

# Define the time boundaries for the last two full months.
time_boundaries = Ewallet.CALCULATE(
    start_period=DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-2 months'),
    end_period=DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
)

# Step 1: Filter transactions for the relevant period and sender type, and extract the transaction day.
transactions_in_period = time_boundaries.Transactions.WHERE(
    (created_at >= start_period) &
    (created_at < end_period) &
    (sender_type == 0)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_day=DATETIME(created_at, 'start of day')
)

# Step 2: Group by day to calculate Daily Active Users (DAU).
daily_active_users = transactions_in_period.PARTITION(name="daily_groups", by=(transaction_day)).CALCULATE(
    transaction_day=transaction_day,
    dau=NDISTINCT(Transactions.sender_id)
)

# Step 3: Group the daily results by month to calculate the average DAU, which is the PMDAU.
pmdau_by_month = daily_active_users.CALCULATE(
    transaction_month=DATETIME(transaction_day, 'start of month')
).PARTITION(name="monthly_groups", by=(transaction_month)).CALCULATE(
    month=transaction_month,
    pmdau=AVG(daily_groups.dau)
)

result = pmdau_by_month

# Get the start of the previous month
prev_month_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-1 day', 'start of month')

# Get the start of the current month (which is the end boundary for previous month)
current_month_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')

# Filter transactions from the previous month
prev_month_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (created_at >= prev_month_start) & 
    (created_at < current_month_start)
)

# Calculate STR
result = Ewallet.CALCULATE(
    str_percentage = 100.0 * COUNT(prev_month_transactions.WHERE(status == 'success')) / COUNT(prev_month_transactions),
    successful_transactions = COUNT(prev_month_transactions.WHERE(status == 'success')),
    total_transactions = COUNT(prev_month_transactions)
)

# Define the start of the previous and current months to create a time window
start_of_prev_month = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-1 month')
start_of_curr_month = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')

# Filter all transactions to include only those from the previous month
prev_month_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (created_at >= start_of_prev_month) & (created_at < start_of_curr_month)
)

# Calculate the Success Transaction Rate (STR)
str_calculation = Ewallet.CALCULATE(
    successful_transactions_count=COUNT(prev_month_transactions.WHERE(status == 'success')),
    total_transactions_count=COUNT(prev_month_transactions)
).CALCULATE(
    previous_month_str=IFF(total_transactions_count > 0, 100.0 * successful_transactions_count / total_transactions_count, 0.0)
)

active_user_transactions_2023 = Users.WHERE(
    status == "active"
).transactions_sent.WHERE(
    (YEAR(created_at) == 2023) & 
    (status == "success") &
    (sender_type == 1)  # Assuming 1 represents user type
).CALCULATE(
    user_id=sender_id,
    year=YEAR(created_at),
    month=MONTH(created_at)
)

# Group by year and month, count distinct users
monthly_active_users = active_user_transactions_2023.PARTITION(
    name="months", 
    by=(year, month)
).CALCULATE(
    month_date=DATETIME(JOIN_STRINGS("-", year, LPAD(month, 2, "0"), "01")),
    num_active_users=NDISTINCT(months.user_id)
).ORDER_BY(month_date.ASC())

result = monthly_active_users

# First, define a contextless expression to filter for transactions that were
# received by a merchant within the last 150 days.
recent_merchant_transactions = transactions_received.WHERE(
    (receiver_type == 1) & (DATEDIFF("days", created_at, 'now') <= 150)
)

# Next, start with the Merchants collection and filter for those that have at least one
# relevant transaction. Then, calculate the total number of transactions and the total
# transaction amount for each merchant. Finally, select the top 2 merchants based on
# the total transaction amount in descending order.
top_merchants_by_amount = Merchants.WHERE(HAS(recent_merchant_transactions) == 1).CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=name,
    total_num_transactions=COUNT(recent_merchant_transactions),
    total_transaction_amount=SUM(recent_merchant_transactions.amount)
).TOP_K(2, by=total_transaction_amount.DESC())

# First, filter the UserSettingSnapshots to include only those from the year 2023.
snapshots_in_2023 = UserSettingSnapshots.WHERE(YEAR(snapshot_date) == 2023)

# Then, identify all snapshots that occurred on the earliest date in 2023.
# This is done by ranking the snapshots by date and selecting all records with rank 1, allowing for ties.
earliest_snapshots_2023 = snapshots_in_2023.WHERE(
    RANKING(by=snapshot_date.ASC(), allow_ties=True) == 1
)

# Finally, calculate the average of the daily and monthly transaction limits for these earliest snapshots.
# The aggregation is performed at the top 'Ewallet' level to produce a single result.
result = Ewallet.CALCULATE(
    avg_tx_limit_daily=AVG(earliest_snapshots_2023.tx_limit_daily),
    avg_tx_limit_monthly=AVG(earliest_snapshots_2023.tx_limit_monthly)
)

[DEBUG] [Q123] Code generated by gemini:
# This contextless expression identifies the single earliest coupon for each merchant.
# RANKING is used with per="Merchants" to rank coupons by their start_date in ascending order for each merchant individually.
# We filter for rank 1 and use SINGULAR() because we need to access the properties of this single earliest coupon.
earliest_coupon = Merchants.coupons.WHERE(
    RANKING(by=start_date.ASC(), per="Merchants", allow_ties=True) == 1
).SINGULAR()

# The main query starts by filtering merchants to find those who meet the specified criteria.
# 1. HAS(coupons) == 1: Ensures we only consider merchants that have at least one coupon.
# 2. DATEDIFF(...) < 1: Checks if the difference in years between the merchant's registration date (created_at)
#    and their earliest coupon's start date is less than 1.
# Finally, CALCULATE selects the required information for the output.
result = Merchants.WHERE(
    (HAS(coupons) == 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("years", created_at, earliest_coupon.start_date) < 1)
).CALCULATE(
    merchant_id=mid,
    registration_date=created_at,
    earliest_coupon_id=earliest_coupon.cid,
    earliest_coupon_start_date=earliest_coupon.start_date
)
